# Fact ledger: /buyers/gated-communities/ (Gated communities in Myrtle Beach)

Researched 2026-10-13 for batch 2026-10-b, page 2. One row per claim. The writer may use rows marked `verified` only. The researcher filled columns 1 to 7. The verifier fills 8 and 9. Every row is "(added 2026-10-13)".

## Verification summary (2026-10-13)

The verifier did none of the research. Every source below was opened again on 2026-10-13. Beach Pro Team pages were fetched one every 10 seconds (its robots.txt crawl delay). Real Geeks was read through `/property/<MLS#>/` pages only. No Zillow, Realtor.com or Redfin page was opened.

**Counts:** 76 rows. Verified 68. Wrong 4 (rows 25, 44, 48, 56). Unverifiable 4 (rows 52 to 55, Georgetown County: the county server was unreachable).

**Rows the writer may not use as written:** 25 (Pawleys Plantation is not shown gated), 44 (Dye Estates price includes a builder sale), 48 (Waterway Palms price: builder rule applied unevenly; use "about $780,000"), 52 to 55 (Georgetown prices, not re-pulled), 56 (The Reserve price: one deed counted twice).

**What was re-done**

- Gate documents: the Municode text (rows 1 to 3), the Litchfield by the Sea FAQ, guide and barcode form (rows 4 to 7), both Barefoot Resort association pages (rows 8 to 10), the Toll Brothers page (row 11), the Myrtle Trace gate PDF (row 13), and AcclaimWeb index entries 4174/75 and 4786/2971 (rows 15, 16).
- Listings: all 138 listings in the evidence tables were re-read on RE/MAX Southern Shores (gate field, fee, golf field). 99 of them were also re-opened on Beach Pro Team (at least 3 per community; every Grande Dunes listing; every listing quoted in rows 17 to 34), plus 3 Seasons listings for row 14. Counts and fees match the ledger everywhere.
- Prices, Horry County: all 30,339 deeds recorded 10/1/2025 to 9/30/2026 were pulled from AcclaimWeb (same total as the method note), matched to fresh county parcel sets, and the medians and quartiles recomputed. All 153 counted deeds in rows 40 to 50 were opened one by one. The counted lists match. Two exclusion errors are in rows 44 and 48.
- Prices, Georgetown County: not possible (see rows 52 to 56).
- Drive minutes: all 19 OSRM routes (rows 57 to 75) re-run from the named starts; all match. Starts were checked in the county address layer (Horry) or the Census geocoder (Georgetown and two others).
- Gate documents in the deed index: the comments search "GATE" does work (it matches memos that start with GATE). It returns only Seaside Plantation 4174/75, Bellwood Landing 4786/2971 and a private gate and dock easement (4255/25). The researcher's note that a search on "GATE" fails is out of date.

**Per community.** "Gate verified": yes = a document from the association, the builder or the county; partly = only some sections or entrances; listings only = MLS fields and listing directions (seller's agent entered, not the association).

That is 16 communities fully named, 3 named in part (Barefoot, Grande Dunes, Myrtle Trace) and 2 left off (Pawleys Plantation, Indigo Bay). The page keeps at least 8 communities in at least 3 areas.

**Four stretches**

- **Group (which sections are gated).** Barefoot: 8 of 26 neighborhoods labeled; the association also says only "some" townhome communities and low-rise entrances are gated, so an unlabeled neighborhood is not shown ungated. Grande Dunes: the builder ties the staffed gate to Cascadia only; listings are mixed and disagree inside Members Club (2 of 3) and Seville (1 of 2). The 19 Del Webb at Grande Dunes listings show no gate. Tidewater has a "Bluffs side" in one listing's directions. Indigo Bay and Pawleys Plantation: gating is shown for part of the listings only.
- **Time.** Municode is Supplement 88 (the latest). The Litchfield by the Sea guide is the 2026 guide (created 2026-06-25); the commercial form is the 2025 form. The Myrtle Trace policy is a 2025 document. The Seaside rules are from 2019 and the Bellwood policy from 2024, and neither text is posted. Georgetown County dates run to 2026-12-29, so its table cannot be trusted without a deed check. The state beach access records were last updated between 2013 and 2019. Listings are active listings as of 2026-10-13.
- **Completeness (some entrances stated as all).** Myrtle Trace: back gate only. Prestwick: a main gate and a side gate. Bellwood Landing: a main gate and a delivery and construction entrance. Wachesaw East: a main gate and a card-key back gate. Litchfield by the Sea: three staffed gates, and guests at Willbrook Plantation, Reserve and Reunion Hall follow the host's instructions (row 5). Rows 1 to 3 cover unincorporated Horry County only: Prestwick, Bellwood Landing, Covington Lake East, Waterway Palms, The Bluffs and Indigo Bay are in it; Tidewater, Seaside, Barefoot and Grande Dunes are in a city.
- **Firmness (a listing field stated as a rule).** "Gated", "GatedCommunity", "guarded", "call box", "business card", "card key only" and the golf field are what seller's agents typed. Say "listings show", never "is". Row 22's card key line and row 30's buzz-in line each rest on one listing. HOA fees are seller-entered; $308 at The Bluffs is twice $154.

**Safety, crime and residents.** No row makes a safety, crime or resident claim, and none draws a conclusion about a named HOA or builder. Things to keep off the page: the words "secure", "exclusive", "luxury" and "upscale" in the Barefoot quotes; "protect our residents" in the Myrtle Trace quote (row 13); "Security" in a listing's HOA includes line; the surname in the Waterway Palms buzz-in quote (row 30 and its evidence table: delete it from the ledger); builder names in the price quotes (rows 44 to 50); the "55 and older" label unless taken from the batch 2026-10-a rows that verified it (rows 13, 14, 51, 76).

---

## Deciding fact (reported first)

The page needs at least 8 communities, in at least three areas, whose gate is shown by a source we can trust. **Count found: 21 communities in 7 areas:** North Myrtle Beach (Barefoot Resort, Seaside Plantation, Tidewater Plantation), Myrtle Beach (Grande Dunes, Prestwick), Carolina Forest (Covington Lake East, Waterway Palms Plantation, The Bluffs on the Waterway, Indigo Bay), Murrells Inlet (Seasons at Prince Creek West, Bellwood Landing, Wachesaw Plantation, Wachesaw East), Pawleys Island and Litchfield (Litchfield by the Sea, The Reserve, Heritage Plantation, Pawleys Plantation, Litchfield Plantation, Willbrook Plantation), DeBordieu Colony near Georgetown, and Conway (Myrtle Trace, back gate only). No gated community was found inside the Town of Surfside Beach; Prestwick is the closest (its nearest public beach access is in Surfside Beach, row 57).

By the strength of the source:

- **The association's own site or document (3):** Barefoot Resort, named neighborhoods only (rows 8 and 9); Litchfield by the Sea (rows 4 to 7); Myrtle Trace, back gate only (row 13, verified in batch 2026-10-a).
- **The builder's own page (1):** the Cascadia section of Grande Dunes, inside a "24-hours a day, staff-gated section" (row 11). Listings add which other Grande Dunes sections are gated (row 34).
- **A gate document recorded with the county, plus listings (2):** Seaside Plantation (rows 15 and 17) and Bellwood Landing (rows 16 and 18). The county shows only the index entry, so the rules themselves are not known.
- **Two allowed listing sites agreeing, marked as listings (15):** Seasons at Prince Creek West (row 14, verified in batch 2026-10-a), Prestwick, Tidewater Plantation, Wachesaw Plantation, Wachesaw East, The Reserve, Heritage Plantation, Pawleys Plantation, Litchfield Plantation, Willbrook Plantation, DeBordieu Colony, Covington Lake East, Waterway Palms Plantation, The Bluffs on the Waterway, Indigo Bay (rows 17 to 34).

Checked and **not** shown gated by listings: Wild Wing Plantation, Del Webb North Myrtle Beach, Plantation Lakes and Del Webb at Grande Dunes (rows 35 to 38).

## How to open the sources

- **Listing sites (marked as listings, never a primary source).** The Beach Pro Team, ERA Real Estate Modo, `https://www.beachproteam.com/listings/view/<address-slug>-sc-real-estate-<MLS#>.html` (robots.txt allows /listings/, asks for a 10-second crawl delay, which was honored), and RE/MAX Southern Shores, `https://mbcoastalhomes.realgeeks.com/property/<MLS#>/` (robots.txt names ClaudeBot and Claude-User under "Allow: /"). Both are fed by the Coastal Carolinas MLS. Each listing used is in the evidence tables at the end. "Gated on both sites" means the RE/MAX Southern Shores field "Amenities" includes "Gated" and the Beach Pro Team fields "Assoc Amenities" include "Gated" or "Security Features" include "GatedCommunity". The listing search used is `https://mbcoastalhomes.realgeeks.com/search/results/?city=all&neighborhood=<MLS neighborhood>&type=all&list_price_min=all&list_price_max=all&area_min=all&beds_min=all&baths_min=all`, which shows listings for sale on 2026-10-13. Never Zillow, Realtor.com or Redfin listing pages.
- **Register of Deeds (AcclaimWeb).** Open https://acclaimweb.horrycounty.org/AcclaimWeb/ and accept the conditions. A Book/Page opens at `https://acclaimweb.horrycounty.org/AcclaimWeb/details/JumpToBookPage/1/<book>/<page>`. The county posts the index entry, not the document text.
- **Sale prices, Horry County.** The method of batch 2026-10-a (seasons ledger row 73): every DEED (001) recorded 10/1/2025 to 9/30/2026 (AcclaimWeb search by Doc Type, month by month; 30,339 deeds countywide, the same total row 73 verified) whose index parcel number is one of the community's parcels in Horry County GIS layer 24 (`https://www.horrycounty.org/parcelapp/rest/services/HorryCountyGISApp/MapServer/24/query?where=<filter>&outFields=PIN,LegalDescr,LandUseCode&returnGeometry=false&f=json`, filter given in each row). Not counted: consideration under $10,000, corrective or re-recorded deeds, deeds on parcels the county codes as vacant land (land use 100), and deeds whose seller is a builder or company named in the row (reported apart). Quartiles use the inclusive method. A check: this code reproduces seasons ledger row 73 and myrtle-trace ledger row 81 exactly (19 sales, median $499,000; 35 sales, median $300,000).
- **Sale prices, Georgetown County.** AcclaimWeb covers Horry County only. For Georgetown County the county's own open data has a parcel table with the last sale on each parcel: `https://gis1.georgetowncountysc.org/portal/rest/services/GCGIS_OpenData/FeatureServer/7/query?where=<filter>&outFields=*&f=json` (layer "PARCELATTRIBUTES", fields SaleDate, SalePrice, LandUseCode, LegalDescription). It holds only the **last** sale of each parcel and no seller name, so a home sold twice counts once and builder sales cannot be taken out. Rows using it say so. Houses are land use codes ending in 100 or 101.
- **Drive times.** OSRM public router (OpenStreetMap roads), fastest route, no traffic. End points: S.C. Department of Environmental Services beach access layer, `https://gis.des.sc.gov/gisserver/rest/services/BeachAccess/MapServer/0/query?where=OBJECTID%3D<id>&outFields=*&returnGeometry=true&outSR=4326&f=json`, records with ACC_TYPE "PUBLIC BEACH ACCESS" or "BEACH PARK" and PARKING "Yes" (a car must be able to park). For each start, an OSRM table to the 40 nearest such accesses by straight line; the fastest kept, then routed alone. Minutes up to 10 are rounded to the nearest minute, over 10 to the nearest 5 (the rule used in batch 2026-10-a). **Start point:** the county address point nearest the average position of the community's home address points (Horry County Addresses layer 22, by the PINs in the price row), or for Georgetown County the home parcel nearest the average of the home parcel centers (layer 2, `https://gis1.georgetowncountysc.org/portal/rest/services/GCGIS_OpenData/FeatureServer/2`). Each row names its start address.
- **County code.** Horry County Code of Ordinances, Chapter 18 (Land Development Regulations), Municode, Supplement 88 (codified through Ordinance No. 37-2026, adopted June 16, 2026). Library page: `https://library.municode.com/sc/horry_county/codes/code_of_ordinances?nodeId=COOR_CH18LADERE`. The text was read through Municode's content service, `https://api.municode.com/CodesContent?jobId=494166&nodeId=COOR_CH18LADERE_ART4DEST_S2ST&productId=10129`.

## Fair housing and claims

No row says anything about safety, crime or who lives anywhere. Listing text that says "secure", "safe" or describes residents was not recorded. No row draws a conclusion about a named HOA or builder. Guard hours from listings are not recorded as fact (seasons ledger row 94 sets the rule).

| 77 | (added 2026-10-13, round 2) South Carolina's State Law Enforcement Division (SLED) publishes crime data that a user can search by jurisdiction (the police department or sheriff), year and category, on its SC Crime Data page (the Theme-Oriented Public Site, TOPS, drawn from the state's incident reporting repository, SCIBRS). The page links to it only; it states no crime figure and rates no neighborhood. | link only | SLED, SC Crime Data page | https://www.sled.sc.gov/sc-crime-data | "SLED has collaborated with Beyond 20/20 to create a web-based extension to the SCIBRS repository. This extension, known as the “Theme-Oriented Public Site,” or TOPS, provides data visualization directly from the SCIBRS repository. Users can search for dynamic crime information by jurisdiction, year, and category." | 2026-10-13 | 2027-04 | verified | Re-opened sled.sc.gov/sc-crime-data 2026-10-13: the quote matches word for word (SCIBRS, the Theme-Oriented Public Site, TOPS, and "by jurisdiction, year, and category"). The page's "Visit TOPS" link goes to https://beyond2020.sled.sc.gov/tops/ (opened, status 200, title "South Carolina Crime Statistics"): its menus are jurisdiction (statewide plus city police, county sheriffs and other agencies), year (1991 to 2024, so the newest year posted is 2024) and theme (Crimes Against Persons, Crimes Against Property, Drug Law Violations/DUI and others). SLED's page says TOPS data is posted after quality review; neither page states how complete the reporting is. LIMIT: the menus choose an agency, a year and a type. There is no address or neighborhood search, and neither page says there is. The page's "near any address" is therefore wrong (see summary). Safe wording: "The state posts reported-crime data that anyone can look up by police department or sheriff, year and type of crime [SLED SC Crime Data page, accessed 2026-10-13]". The newest year is 2024 [TOPS menu, accessed 2026-10-13]. |

It is a map of CALLS FOR SERVICE, not crimes: the app's headings are "GIS Calls For Service Application", "Calls For Service" and "Call Categories"; the call types in the data include medical, alarms, animal, 911 hang-up, welfare check, suspicious person, noise, found property and "KEEP CHECK" as well as assault and larceny. ARITHMETIC: recomputed from the JSON: 33 sales, low $183,000, high $2,178,000, median $645,000, middle half $340,000 to $1,045,000. Correct. LISTINGS: opened all 33 (and the 5 in row 91) on mbcoastalhomes.realgeeks.com/property/<MLS#>/ (robots.txt allows Claude-User; /property/ is not disallowed; I did not open Zillow). Every price, closing date (all inside 2025-10-01 to 2026-09-30), type and status CLOSED matches the JSON. All show Region 41a Pawleys Island Area-Litchfield Beaches and Amenities Gated. Neighborhood field names the section; 31 of 33 descriptions say Litchfield or LBTS; 2517518 and 2600059 (Summerhouse at LBTS) do not in the text but the MLS neighborhood is Summerhouse at LBTS. ARE THESE SECTIONS PART OF THE COMMUNITY: yes, all of them. The association's own site (litchfieldbythesea.com/communities/ and /community/, litchfieldbythesea.org is a parked page) lists 30 sub-associations. It names Bridgewater, Seaside Inn, Summerhouse, Lakeside, Sandpiper Run (its page covers Sandpiper Run and the Crescent), Somerset and Breakers Reef. Cambridge and Warwick are NOT separate sections: the Somerset page says Somerset has the buildings Fordham, Whitney Parrish, Warwick, Hamilton, Cambridge and Paget (listings 2513333 and 2610234 also say Somerset). So the nine MLS section names are seven association sub-associations. Do not write nine sections. Caveats: Seaside Inn is a hotel-style property (association: guest rooms and suites; the sale 2606829 is a condo with lockout), and Summerhouse is 98 one-bedroom units run with the resort; both are on the association list. MISSING SECTIONS (the sampling gap is real): I searched the MLS by the association's other section names and found 10 more whole-unit condo closings in the same window that the scan missed: Heron Marsh (5: 2508168 $540,000, 2511481 $595,000, 2523404 $600,000, 2605779 $610,000, 2606229 $610,000), Marsh Hawk (2: 2524029 $535,000, 2605250 $570,000), Fordham (2528976 $1,385,000), Hamilton (2518154 $1,050,000), Shipyard Village (2524131 $750,000). One more, Magnolia Run 2606637 $394,000, is a River Club golf villa whose text does not name Litchfield by the Sea, so I did not count it. The Paget and Whitney Parrish buildings returned nothing under the names I tried. With the 10 the count is 43 and the middle sale is $610,000 (middle half $513,500 to $1,022,500); the low and the high do not change. The pooled middle moves with which sections were found, and the pool is two groups (9 sales under $400,000, 10 at $1,000,000 or more), so a single middle figure is not safe. EXCLUDED SALES: spot-checked 2610727, 2500435, 2528694, 2614798, 2614067, 2522062, 2615776: all say interval or shared-week ownership. 2613816 and 2613853 (125 South Dunes, no description, fee $5,311) are unclear and stay out. A page that says condos sold from about $180,000 must say whole condos, because shared-week units sold from $19,500 to $220,000. SAFE WORDING (page may not say its condos as a whole): 'Whole-unit condos in the sections we counted sold for about $180,000 to $2.2 million between October 2025 and September 2026 [Coastal Carolinas MLS closed sales, 33 in 7 sections, with at least 10 more in other sections not in that count]'. The extremes are firm for what was found; the middle is not. If a middle is wanted: 'about $600,000 to $650,000 in the middle, in the sections we counted' is the most the data supports, and I would not use 'usually'. PAGE CHANGES: paragraph: replace 'Over the last 12 months, its condos sold for about $180,000 to $2.2 million.' with 'In the sections we counted, whole condos (not shared weeks) sold for about $180,000 to $2.2 million over the last 12 months.' Table cell: replace 'Condos about $180,000 to $2.2 million' with 'Condos in the sections we counted: about $180,000 to $2.2 million'. Do not use nine. |

| 101 | (added 2026-10-14, round 7) Prestwick: an 18-hole golf course between the homes. The owners association owns a large pool, a smaller pool and a tennis center with clay courts and two hard courts; owners use the pool and the hard courts. Golf and the clay courts take a separate membership. | as listed | Club + Resort Business (citing Myrtle Beach Online); MLS listing descriptions; OpenStreetMap | https://clubandresortbusiness.com/prestwick-cc-poa-keeps-tennis-swim-facilities-going/ ; https://mbcoastalhomes.realgeeks.com/property/<MLS#>/ ; https://www.openstreetmap.org/relation/3973684 | Article: "The POA purchased the facilities, which include 11 clay courts and two hard courts, a tennis pro shop, locker facilities, a pavilion with restrooms, a half Olympic-size swimming pool and a smaller pool" "the pool facility and the two hard courts are now available to Prestwick's more than 600 homeowners, and the tennis club program has special membership rates for residents to use the other 11 clay courts." 2624428: "an award-winning 18-hole golf course, community pool and clay tennis courts"; 2620425: "The HOA includes use of the large pool and facilities" "Golf, tennis and social memberships are available for purchase as a separate fee from the monthly HOA dues"; 2526904: "The golf course and tennis are a separate membership." OSM: Prestwick Country Club (relation 3973684). Evidence: batches/2026-10-b/data/gated-amenities-mls.json | 2026-10-14 | 2027-04 | wrong | 2026-10-14, round 7 independent verifier. WRONG: "Golf and the clay courts take a separate membership". The club's own site (prestwickcountryclub.com, 1001 Links Road): "Rated Top Public Course! ... has the look and feel of a private club, but is open to the public". Club + Resort Business (2016-02-16, the date is on the page): the owner "made the golf course solely a daily-fee course on December 31" and "was doing away with memberships". Public tee times are posted for 2026. So golf is a daily-fee course, not a membership. Five of the 17 listings (2620425, 2526904, 2602370, 2601394, 2528757) say golf is a separate or optional membership; they conflict with the club's site and are out of date. (Do not confuse with prestwickcc.com, an Illinois club.) STANDS: 18-hole Pete Dye course; large pool and smaller pool; 11 clay courts and 2 hard courts; clay courts need a tennis club membership (article, 2016; 8 listings 2026 say tennis or tennis stadium membership is separate). Listings 2622303 and 2620425 (2026) say residents have the pool; the article (2016) says the pool and the 2 hard courts are open to homeowners. CORRECT: "The owners association owns": the article says both "purchased" and a lease to a third-party operator; ownership today is not shown. Safe wording: "Prestwick has an 18-hole golf course between the homes, open to the public by the round [club site]. Its tennis center has clay courts and two hard courts, and it has a large pool and a smaller pool [Club + Resort Business, 2016; listings 2026]. The clay courts take a separate tennis membership [same]". Trade-off line: "the clay tennis courts cost extra, through a separate membership; golf is paid by the round". Researcher note: The article is undated on its page; it follows the club's 2015 announcement (linked from it). 2026 listings agree on the pool, the courts and separate memberships. |
| 102 | (added 2026-10-14, round 7) Seasons at Prince Creek West: 444 home lots; every home must have a resident 55 or older; the clubhouse has indoor and outdoor pools, a fitness center, tennis and bocce; the HOA fee includes internet, weekly trash pickup and lawn care; it is part of Prince Creek West, which has a 43-acre park. | as in batch 2026-10-a | Batch 2026-10-a Seasons ledger rows 19, 30 and 56 (verified); registry seasons-hoa-fee | batches/2026-10-a/facts/seasons-at-prince-creek-west-facts.md ; facts/registry.json | See those rows. | 2026-10-14 | 2027-04 | verified with corrected wording | 2026-10-14, round 7 independent verifier. Matches batch 2026-10-a Seasons rows 19, 30, 56 and registry seasons-hoa-fee, with their limits. CORRECT: "Every home must have a resident 55 or older": Seasons row 5 says "Every occupied home". CORRECT: the fee line. Batch a row 88 allows only "some listings say the fee includes internet, weekly trash pickup and lawn care" (4 of 8 descriptions; MLS field: internet 6 of 8, trash 7, grounds 7); row 36: the charter makes the association responsible for grass cutting and landscaping maintenance, and irrigation is an owner duty. Say "The HOA fee is shown on listings as including internet, trash pickup and grounds care"; drop "weekly" and "irrigation". Row 56 is a 2024-01-31 press release; the amenity list is two years old, so it may change by 2027-01. Row 30: the 43.223 acre park is owned by the road and park association; "owns" is inferred from the deed index. Photo label: parcel center from Horry County layer 24 stands; the aerial shows a large building, an outdoor pool and tennis courts there. Researcher note: Already verified. The photo label is the clubhouse parcel center (Horry County parcels layer 24, PIN 46802030007, 33.586192, -79.071152). |

The marina is on the Waccamaw River and the Intracoastal Waterway (1 listing; the other says Waccamaw River). | 108 | (added 2026-10-14, round 7) Litchfield Plantation is on an old rice plantation near Pawleys Island; the road in is lined with centuries-old live oaks, the Avenue of Oaks. Its homes are houses and townhouses. Owners can use the plantation house, built in the 1700s, a pool that looks out over the old rice fields, and an oceanfront beach house with parking on Pawleys Island. A private marina in the plantation rents boat slips on the Waccamaw River for an extra fee. | as listed | MLS listing descriptions and fields | https://mbcoastalhomes.realgeeks.com/property/<MLS#>/ | 2625001: "travel beneath the centuries-old live oaks ... along the iconic Avenue of Oaks" "access to the historic plantation house, a beautiful community pool overlooking the former rice fields, and a private oceanfront beach house on Pawleys Island" "a privately owned marina within the plantation offers access to the Waccamaw River and Intracoastal Waterway"; 2624734: "use of the original 1750 Plantation House, a community pool overlooking the ricefields, and access to the community-owned oceanfront beach house on Pawleys Island"; 2620130: "its own private beach house with bathrooms and parking" "boat storage and a marina available for an additional fee"; 2620128: "the 200-to 300-year-old moss-laden live oaks lining the Avenue of the Oaks". Types read: 11 Detached, 6 Townhouse, 1 Condominium. Row 26: gate house on the Avenue of Live Oaks. Evidence: batches/2026-10-b/data/gated-amenities-mls.json | 2026-10-14 | 2027-04 | verified with corrected wording | 2026-10-14, round 7 independent verifier. Read all 18 descriptions and the MLS types (11 detached, 6 townhouse, 1 condominium). Avenue of Oaks with old live oaks: 5 listings. Community pool overlooking the rice fields: 13 listings. Oceanfront beach house on Pawleys Island: 17 listings; parking at it: 3 listings (one gives 148 Atlantic Avenue). Plantation house: 6 listings; "1750" in 1, "1740" in the Wikipedia article, so "built in the 1700s" stands. CORRECT: "rents boat slips": no listing says slips. The wording is "boat storage and a marina available for an additional fee" (1), "optional private marina" (2), "small marina" (1), and the marina is on the Waccamaw River (3) or the Intracoastal Waterway (1). CORRECT: "Owners can use the plantation house": listings say "available for special events" (2) and "use of" (1). CORRECT: "houses and townhouses" leaves out 1 condominium listing; say "mostly houses and townhouses". Safe wording: "Litchfield Plantation is on an old rice plantation near Pawleys Island. The road in is lined with old live oaks, the Avenue of Oaks. Its homes are mostly houses and townhouses. It has a plantation house built in the 1700s, a pool that looks out over the old rice fields and an oceanfront beach house with parking on Pawleys Island. | 109 | (added 2026-10-14, round 7) Litchfield by the Sea: more than a mile of beach; houses on the ocean, villas by the marsh and condos; an oceanfront Beach Club with a deck and a pavilion; pools; tennis and pickleball courts near the Beach Club; fishing and crabbing docks on Osprey Lake; paved paths through the marsh and woods. | as quoted | Litchfield by the Sea home page and amenities page | https://litchfieldbythesea.com/ ; https://litchfieldbythesea.com/amenities/ | Home: "private beach access, where over a mile of pristine Atlantic shoreline meets a magnificent oceanfront Beach Club. This central hub features an expansive sundeck and pavilion" "championship tennis and pickleball courts, a private pool, and tranquil fishing docks and crabbing piers" "Miles of paved scenic trails wind through preserved marshlands and maritime forests" "housing options, which range from luxurious oceanfront estates to charming marsh-view villas and sophisticated condominiums". Amenities: "Fishing and Crabbing Docks ... along Osprey Lake" "Private courts near the Beach Club". Rows 79 and 80 (verified) carry the beach and Beach Club. | 2026-10-14 | 2027-04 | verified with corrected wording | 2026-10-14, round 7 independent verifier. Opened litchfieldbythesea.com home, amenities pages and each amenity page, and the 2026 guest guide PDF. Beach Club with deck and pavilion: home page "sundeck and pavilion". Tennis and pickleball courts near the Beach Club, fishing and crabbing docks along Osprey Lake, paved paths: match. CORRECT: "more than a mile of beach": the home page says "over a mile", but the Beach Club page says "one mile of unforgettable Atlantic shoreline". Say "about a mile". CORRECT: "pools": the amenity pages name one pool, the River Club Pool (2093 Club Circle); the home page says "a private pool"; only the guest guide says "community pools". Say "a pool at the River Club" unless the owner confirms more. CORRECT: "villas by the marsh": the site says "marsh-view villas". Say "villas with marsh views". "Houses on the ocean": the site says "oceanfront estates". Safe wording: "Litchfield by the Sea has about a mile of beach, an oceanfront Beach Club with a deck and a pavilion, a pool at the River Club, tennis and pickleball courts near the Beach Club, fishing and crabbing docks on Osprey Lake and paved paths through the marsh and woods. Quotes match: "a gated Lowcountry community nestled between the Waccamaw River and the beautiful beaches of Pawleys Island"; "500 custom homes"; "four tennis courts, four pickleball courts, a pool and an owners' clubhouse with a fitness room"; "less than three miles from the ocean"; "freshwater lakes and marshes". The site calls it "marina-anchored" and does not say the marina is on the river; "a marina" is the safe wording, "on the river" is the researcher's inference. | 112 | (added 2026-10-14, round 7) Aerial photos on this page: USDA NAIP 2023 views exported from the USGS National Map image service, each labelled at a Census-geocoded address from rows 57 to 76 (Litchfield by the Sea: 12 Riptide Ln, row 91; Seasons: its clubhouse parcel). Captions say only what each photo shows. | 18 photos | USGS National Map NAIP image service; U.S. Census Bureau geocoder | https://imagery.nationalmap.gov/arcgis/rest/services/USGSNAIPImagery/ImageServer | Records with each export request, tile names, flight date and label coordinates: batches/2026-10-b/data/photos.json (slug gated-communities, files images/buyers/gated-communities/*-from-above-2023.webp). | 2026-10-14 | 2027-04 | verified with corrected wording | 2026-10-14, round 7 independent verifier. Opened all 20 images (18 NAIP 2023, 2 NOAA 2026) and viewed each. Labels sit on the place in all of them (single home addresses, the Seasons clubhouse parcel, the coast, the gatehouse and gate arm); each label is a point, not the neighborhood edge. Captions to change: (1) Seaside: "two freshwater lakes" shows at least three bodies of water and fresh or salt is not visible. Say "houses beside lakes". (2) Covington Lake East: "around its lakes" shows ponds. Say "with ponds among them". (3) Waterway Palms: "the clubhouse and pools in the middle": the picture shows a large building, two pool shapes and two tennis courts on a point in the water at the left of centre. Say "a large building, pools and tennis courts on a point in the water at the left". (4) Heritage: "its pool and tennis courts near the label" claims ownership. Say "a pool and tennis courts near the label". (5) Prestwick gate: "Just inside, a small building ... sits between": the picture does not show inside or outside. Say "A small building, the gatehouse, is between the two lanes, with a gate arm across each lane." (6) DeBordieu: "between most of them and the beach": homes also line the beach and the marsh edge; say "a salt marsh is between many of the homes and the beach". (7) Litchfield by the Sea: "condo buildings" are large buildings in the picture; say "large buildings by the beach". All other captions say only what the picture shows. Researcher note: Check each caption against its photo. |

| 114 | (added 2026-10-14, round 8) Tidewater Golf Club: Ken Tomlinson, a Columbia lawyer who also designed courses, found a 600-acre site on the Intracoastal Waterway; Hurricane Hugo (1989) delayed the work and the course opened in 1990; Golf Digest and Golf Magazine both named it the best new public course in America that year. Holes 3 and 12 play over the waterway's marsh, facing Cherry Grove. | as stated | Discover South Carolina (the state tourism department), article by Bob Gillespie; Golf Digest course page | https://discoversouthcarolina.com/articles/the-amateurs-touch-columbia-attorney-built-one-of-myrtle-beachs-finest-courses ; https://www.golfdigest.com/places-to-play/sc/tidewater-golf-club-tidewater | "he found a 600-acre site in North Myrtle Beach, positioned along the Intracoastal Waterway." "In 1990, a year after delays caused by Hurricane Hugo in 1989, Tidewater opened for business." "named by both Golf Digest and Golf Magazine as the best new public course in America in 1990." "the par-3 third hole ... the threat of the Intracoastal Waterway's marshland" "the 12th ... players fire from 145-189 yards over marshlands". Golf Digest reviewers: holes 3, 4, 12 and 13 run along the marsh facing Cherry Grove. | 2026-10-14 | 2027-10 | verified with corrected wording | 2026-10-14, round 8 independent verifier. Opened the state tourism article (Discover South Carolina, Bob Gillespie): Tomlinson "a Columbia-based attorney", "he found a 600-acre site", "In 1990, a year after delays caused by Hurricane Hugo in 1989, Tidewater opened", "named by both Golf Digest and Golf Magazine as the best new public course in America in 1990". A trade paper of the time, Golf Course News (April 1997, archive.lib.msu.edu), says the same: "GOLF and Golf Digest magazines voted the best new public course of the year in 1990", and calls him a "tax attorney turned course architect". The course's own site (tidewatergolf.com) names Ken Tomlinson as architect and says it is a public course. Golf Digest's page returned Access Denied, so its reviewers' notes (holes 3, 4, 12, 13) were NOT read. CORRECT: "Holes 3 and 12 play over the marsh of the waterway, facing Cherry Grove". The state article says hole 3 has the marsh "hard along the left side", hole 12 plays "over marshlands", and hole 4 has "views of Cherry Grove Beach across Cherry Grove Inlet". Safe wording: "Holes 3 and 12 meet the marsh of the waterway, and hole 4 looks across Cherry Grove Inlet to Cherry Grove Beach [Discover South Carolina]". Researcher note: "Columbia lawyer": the article calls him a Columbia-based attorney; a 1997 Golf Course News piece calls him a tax attorney. |

Opened the Horry Independent article (myhorrynews.com, Sept 2023): "The Fall Migration is expected to bring at least 10,000 people to North Myrtle Beach"; "Society of Stranders is hosting its annual Fall Migration celebration for 11 days"; "All of the Main Street venues and businesses along Main Street benefit"; Fun Monday is held on Main Street. The second article returned 429. The Society's own site (shagdance.com) lists the Spring Safari (April) and the Fall Migration (September) with 2026-2028 dates. The paper gives "thousands" only for the fall; no newspaper source was found for the spring crowd (resort pages say thousands). Golf Course Industry (Golfdom, April 2000, archive.lib.msu.edu): Barefoot "opens this month" and "took less than two years to build"; the Golf Trips article (2020) says April 2000. The site gives no month; the trade paper does. CORRECT: "Before that, the land was used for hunting" and "Hurricane Floyd's flooding in 1999 delayed the opening" each come from one interview in one golf-package article (the general manager, 2020). | 117 | (added 2026-10-14, round 8) Grande Dunes was developed by Burroughs & Chapin, the company that grew out of Myrtle Beach Farms (formed in 1912, when Simeon Chapin bought a half-interest in the Burroughs & Collins venture). Its Resort Course, designed by Roger Rulewich, opened in 2001 on a bluff above the Intracoastal Waterway, with 34 acres of lakes. | as stated | South Carolina Encyclopedia (Burroughs); myrtlebeachgolftrips.com (Myrtle Beach Golf Trips, the golf-package site of the area's golf marketing group); GolfPass | https://www.scencyclopedia.org/sce/?p=6428 ; https://www.myrtlebeachgolftrips.com/news/discover-grande-dunes-resort-club-myrtle-beach-golf-at-its-most-spectacular ; https://www.golfpass.com/travel-advisor/articles/grande-dunes-resort-course-in-myrtle-beach-big-bold-intracoastal-waterway-golf | SC Encyclopedia: in 1912 Simeon B. Chapin bought a half-interest; the partnership was named Myrtle Beach Farms, later Burroughs & Chapin. Golf Trips: "Robert Harper, then director of golf operations for Burroughs & Chapin, the course's developer". GolfPass: opened in 2001, designed by Roger Rulewich, on a bluff overlooking the Intracoastal Waterway, 34 acres of lakes. | 2026-10-14 | 2027-10 | verified with corrected wording | 2026-10-14, round 8 independent verifier. Sun News (aol.com copy, "Miami look in Myrtle Beach"): "the project, launched by Burroughs & Chapin"; first sale October 2000 after $100 million of infrastructure. That supports the community, not only the course. Golf Course Industry (April 2000): Grande Dunes is a "Roger Rulewich-designed course scheduled to open next year" (2001). GolfPass: "Opened in 2001 ... Resort Course ... embarked upon by ... Burroughs & Chapin". CORRECT (company history): the cited SC Encyclopedia page (p=6428) does NOT contain the 1912 text; it is on the Myrtle Beach entry (p=8868): "In 1912 Simeon B. Chapin ... bought a half-interest in the Burroughs & Collins Company's Myrtle Beach venture ... The partnership was named Myrtle Beach Farms, later Burroughs & Chapin". SC Public Radio: "Burroughs & Chapin Company, Inc., was formed in 1990 when ... Burroughs & Collins Company of Conway merged with Myrtle Beach Farms Company". CORRECT: "on a bluff ... with 34 acres of lakes" appears only on golf travel sites; the course's own site returned 403. Drop "bluff" and "34 acres". Safe wording: "Grande Dunes was launched by Burroughs & Chapin, formed in 1990 from Myrtle Beach Farms (a partnership begun in 1912) and Burroughs & Collins. Its Resort Course, designed by Roger Rulewich, opened in 2001 beside the Intracoastal Waterway [Sun News; Golf Course Industry]". Grande Dunes was later sold to a Varde Partners and Freehold venture (Builder), so keep the past tense. Researcher note: The Golf Trips article names Burroughs & Chapin as the course's developer; the page says the company developed Grande Dunes. If only the course is supported, the page should say "Burroughs & Chapin built its Resort Course". |
| 118 | (added 2026-10-14, round 8) Carolina Forest was a paper company's pine land (International Paper bought the Buist Tract in 1937), used for the Conway Bombing and Gunnery Range in World War II; its neighborhoods were planned from the late 1990s (county development agreement, December 1997). Waterway Palms Plantation is on the Intracoastal Waterway at its east edge. | as stated | Wikipedia (Carolina Forest, South Carolina), citing Horry County; OpenStreetMap | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carolina_Forest,_South_Carolina | "International Paper bought the roughly 30,000-acre Buist Tract in 1937. It was used as part of the Conway Bombing and Gunnery Range during World War II." "In December 1997, Horry County approved a development agreement covering about 7,073 acres of Carolina Forest." | 2026-10-14 | 2027-10 | verified with corrected wording | 2026-10-14, round 8 independent verifier. Primary and newspaper sources found. Horry Independent: "Fashioned in 1997, the Carolina Forest land development agreement ... passed third reading on Dec. 9, 1997"; International Paper "first proposed the Carolina Forest concept in 1995". Horry Independent (2018): "The former Conway Bombing and Gunnery Range covers more than 55,000 acres, including parts of Carolina Forest. The area was used by the U.S. Army as a range between 1942 and 1947." Post and Courier (2024) says the same and adds that the Army Corps of Engineers has a $12.4 million plan for a 455-acre site in Carolina Forest. "Pine land" and the 1937 Buist Tract purchase are on Wikipedia only; the page does not need them. CORRECT: say "land owned by International Paper", not "a paper company's pine land". "East edge": Wikipedia puts the Intracoastal Waterway on its east side; not checked against a county map. NOTE: Covington Lake began in 1996 per Wikipedia, before the 1997 agreement, so "its neighborhoods were planned from the late 1990s" is loose; say "a county agreement of December 1997 set the plan for its neighborhoods". Safe wording: "Carolina Forest was land owned by International Paper. The Army used part of it as the Conway Bombing and Gunnery Range from 1942 to 1947. Horry County approved a development agreement for it in December 1997". Researcher note: Secondary source. A verifier should find the county's development agreement or a Sun News article before this ships; otherwise cut the history sentence. |

CORRECT: "storage yard": listings say "boat storage" or "boat storage area" only. CORRECT: "a lagoon that winds through the neighborhood" gives the water a motion; the build bans personification. | 121 | (added 2026-10-14, round 8) Covington Lake East is next to Tuckahoe Bay, a Carolina bay; the Covington Lake neighborhoods were started in 1996, among the first in Carolina Forest. | as stated | Wikipedia (Tuckahoe Bay); OpenStreetMap | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tuckahoe_Bay | "Covington Lake was built to the southwest of the bay and Waterford Plantation was built to the northwest. WATCH: the same article says a new pool complex and a new Magnolia's restaurant are under construction, "Completion is slated for the spring of 2027", so "swim at the club pool" (page entry 12) may be out of date for now. Researcher note: The club's own site was refused by the proxy; the founding year should be read there by the verifier. |

Komen International, then City of Hope Myrtle Beach Classic, then Kathy Ireland Greens.com Classic in 2000). On The Green: four LPGA events. The 350-acre Greg Norman design includes 160 acres of natural wetlands". All parts of the sentence match; "350 acres" rests on the tourism page only. |
| 129 | (added 2026-10-14, round 8) Willbrook: part of a 2,400-acre grant to Robert Daniel in 1711; by 1850, 140 enslaved people worked its rice fields; hurricanes from 1889 to 1911 broke the dikes and ended rice growing along the Waccamaw. Dan Maples' course opened in 1988 after an archaeological study; along it stand the chimney of the plantation house, which burned in 1895, and the cemetery of the enslaved people of Oatland Plantation. | as stated | myrtlebeachgolftrips.com (Myrtle Beach Golf Trips, the golf-package site of the area's golf marketing group) (two articles drawn from the course's history markers) | https://www.myrtlebeachgolftrips.com/news/five-historical-items-you-didnt-know-about-willbrook-plantation/ ; https://www.myrtlebeachgolftrips.com/news/willbrook-plantation-golf-club-celebrates-30-years-on-a-land-steeped-in-ric | "Willbrook was part of a 2,400-acre tract granted by the Lords Proprietors to Robert Daniel in 1711" "At the height of rice production in 1850, these cabins housed 140 slaves who toiled daily in the Willbrook rice fields" "the hurricanes of 1889, 1893, 1898, 1906, 1910, and 1911 wrecked the dike system and ended rice production along the Waccamaw River" "In 1988, Willbrook Plantation Golf Club was built on that land, but not until after extensive study" "There's a chimney that still stands at the site of the plantation house, a sign marking the slaves cemetery" "the original Willbrook Plantation house ... surviving until it burned in June 1895" "Oakland Plantation Slave Cemetery (8th hole) ... slaves who labored on Oatland Plantation". | 2026-10-14 | 2027-10 | wrong | 2026-10-14, round 8 independent verifier. WRONG on three numbers and one cause; the rest can stand. Primary source: Chicora Foundation Research Series 31, "Archaeological and historical examinations of three eighteenth and nineteenth century rice plantations on the Waccamaw Neck" (Trinkley, SC State Library), which cites the SC Archives and History 1850 Georgetown Slave Schedule. (1) GRANT: "originally part of a 24,000 acre tract granted by the Lords Proprietors to Landgrave Robert Daniel on June 18, 1711". The course markers (via two golf-package articles) say 2,400; 2,400 is the size of the Willbrook development area ("The 2400 acre Willbrook Plantation area"). (2) ENSLAVED PEOPLE: "In 1850, 149 slaves worked on 375 acres of improved farm land owned by Tucker" (Table 1: Willbrook 149). The marker says 140 housed in the cabins. 149 is the census count. (3) HURRICANES: Chicora lists "the hurricanes of 1893, 1894, 1898, 1906, 1910, and 1911 that wrecked the dike system"; the marker adds 1889. Chicora also names the loss of a stable work force and competition from western rice lands as causes. (4) HOUSE: "By 1895 the presumed original 1798 structure burned and was replaced the next year by a new house ... This 1896 structure remained intact until demolished in 1985." So the standing chimney is not from the house that burned in 1895. STANDS: the course opened in 1988 after an archaeological study (Chicora survey 1987; Memorandum of Agreement of July 23, 1987 for golf-course impacts; Golf Trips: "built in 1988"). The cemetery is from the course marker only (it is headed Oakland, its text says Oatland); Chicora's study does not describe it. Safe wording for the page, plain: "Willbrook was part of a 24,000-acre grant made by the Lords Proprietors to Robert Daniel in 1711. Rice was grown here by enslaved people. The 1850 census counted 149 enslaved people at Willbrook. After the Civil War, the loss of workers, rice grown farther west and hurricanes from 1893 to 1911 that wrecked the dikes ended rice growing on the Georgetown rivers." "Dan Maples' course opened in 1988, after an archaeological study of the land. A marked cemetery of enslaved people and the brick remains of the plantation house, rebuilt after a fire in 1895, are beside the course." Never as a selling point; keep it in the history paragraph. The owner may ask the photo partner for a photo of the cemetery marker. Researcher note: Designer: the course is a Dan Maples design (row 107 listings; Golf Trips "Maples' Dandy Dozen"). |
| 130 | (added 2026-10-14, round 8) Litchfield Plantation began with royal land grants to Thomas Hepworth from 1710 to 1712, and Peter Simons named it; it grew rice on the Waccamaw, and the state's survey of Georgetown County's rice plantations lists it. Its plantation house dates from the 1700s. | as stated | SC Department of Archives and History, SC Historic Property Record S108042003700044; Wikipedia (Litchfield Plantation) | https://schpr.sc.gov/index.php/Detail/objects/S108042003700044 ; https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Litchfield_Plantation | SCDAH record: "Litchfield Plantation", collection "Georgetown County - Rice Culture, 1974-1982". Wikipedia: "three land grants of 500, 500 and 420 acres from King George III to Thomas Hepworth, in 1710, 1712 and 1711. The plantation was named 'Litchfield' by Peter Simon[s]". House: Wikipedia 1740; the state record lists 1794; listings 1740 and 1750. | 2026-10-14 | 2027-10 | wrong | 2026-10-14, round 8 independent verifier. WRONG in part. SCDAH record S108042003700044 (quote): "Litchfield Plantation", "Street Address: Georgetown County", "Date Surveyed: 1982-05-18", "Construction Date: 1794", "Survey: Georgetown County - Rice Culture, 1974-1982". That supports: the state's Rice Culture survey lists it, and a house of the 1700s (the state says 1794; Wikipedia 1740; a listing 1750). NOT supported: the Hepworth grants and Peter Simons naming it rest on Wikipedia only, which says the grants came "from King George III" in 1710 to 1712, impossible (South Carolina was then under the Lords Proprietors, so not royal grants). Chicora Foundation (Research Series 31) only says Captain Thomas Hepworth owned land south of the 1711 Willbrook tract, and that Peter Simons is linked to Litchfield by a 1791 plat but "no records were found of any land holdings on the Waccamaw Neck" for him. Chicora also says the Tucker family consolidated Willbrook and Litchfield. Safe wording: "Litchfield was a rice plantation on the Waccamaw River; the state's Rice Culture survey of Georgetown County lists it [SC Archives and History]. Its plantation house dates from the 1700s. In the 1800s it belonged to the Tucker family, who also owned Willbrook [Chicora Foundation]." Researcher note: "Peter Simons": Wikipedia spells it Simon and Simons in different places. The page says "the 1700s" for the house because the dates differ. |
| 131 | (added 2026-10-14, round 8) Litchfield by the Sea was built by The Litchfield Company, founded in 1956. | as stated | Wikipedia (The Litchfield Company) | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Litchfield_Company | "a real estate development firm founded in 1956 in South Carolina by A. Foster McKissick, James Bernice Moore, and Bill Miller Sr." Litchfield by the Sea is listed among its largest properties. | 2026-10-14 | 2027-10 | wrong | 2026-10-14, round 8 independent verifier. WRONG as sourced: secondary source. Wikipedia (The Litchfield Company) is tagged as needing references and cites none. The company's own profile (LeadingRE, thelitchfieldcompany.com returned 403) says only "Since 1956, The Litchfield Company Real Estate has dedicated itself to serving ... the communities along the South Carolina coast", which is the brokerage, not who built Litchfield by the Sea. Closest primary evidence: Chicora Foundation Research Series 31 (1990) says the study "was funded by The Litchfield Company of South Carolina", which was advancing the Willbrook golf course in 1987, and that title to part of the land passed to "Litchfield by the Sea" in 1984. That shows the company developed Willbrook, not that it built Litchfield by the Sea or was founded in 1956. Comes off the page: "The Litchfield Company, founded in 1956, built Litchfield by the Sea". Safe wording: "Litchfield by the Sea lies between the marsh and the ocean." Ask the owner for the Georgetown County recorded declaration if the developer is wanted. Researcher note: Secondary source; a county plat or a Georgetown Times article would be stronger. |
| 132 | (added 2026-10-14, round 8) Heritage Plantation is on the land of two rice plantations, True Blue and Midway; Dan Maples' Heritage Club course opened there in 1986. DeBordieu: a summer retreat since rice planters built homes there before the Civil War; later owners of the land included Isaac Emerson (Bromo-Seltzer) and the Vanderbilt family; 2,700 acres, at least 600 a nature preserve, 17,000 undeveloped acres border it to the south. | as stated | Heritage Club descriptions (golf travel sites); DeBordieu Club home page | https://www.golfbreaks.com/en-gb/holidays/myrtle-beach/heritage-club/ ; https://www.playgolfmyrtlebeach.com/news/everything-need-know-heritage-club-south-strand-trailblazer ; https://www.debordieuclub.com/ | Heritage: "Built on the site of the True Blue and Midway Plantations" "Opened in 1986 and designed by Dan Maples"; "much of Pawleys' reputation began with the opening of Heritage in 1986". DeBordieu Club: "The affluent owners of these plantations built a colony of homes here, making it their summer retreat." "The land, once owned by 'Bromo-Seltzer King' Dr. Isaac Emerson and later the Vanderbilt family" "At least 600 acres have been designated as a nature preserve, and an additional 17,000 acres of undeveloped land borders DeBordieu to the south." | 2026-10-14 | 2027-10 | verified with corrected wording | 2026-10-14, round 8 independent verifier. DEBORDIEU (the club's own page, debordieuclub.com): "Before the Civil War, the area was home to prosperous rice plantations. The affluent owners of these plantations built a colony of homes here, making it their summer retreat. Though the Civil War brought an end to this era, the late 19th century saw wealthy businessmen from the north drawn to DeBordieu's allure"; "The land, once owned by 'Bromo-Seltzer King' Dr. Isaac Emerson and later the Vanderbilt family"; 2,700 acres; "At least 600 acres have been designated as a nature preserve, and an additional 17,000 acres of undeveloped land borders DeBordieu to the south". CORRECT: "DeBordieu has been a summer retreat since rice planters built homes there before the Civil War" says the colony still runs; the club says that era ended with the war. Safe wording: "Before the Civil War, rice planters built a colony of summer homes at DeBordieu. Later owners of the land included Isaac Emerson, the Bromo-Seltzer maker, and the Vanderbilt family." HERITAGE: the Heritage Club's own page was not found (heritageclub.com is an Ohio club; do not use it). The 1986 opening and Dan Maples: playgolfmyrtlebeach.com (2018, "the opening of Heritage in 1986") and Golfbreaks ("Opened in 1986 and designed by Dan Maples"). The True Blue and Midway plantations: golf and marina pages agree but there is no primary source; the True Blue record places that plantation northwest of US 17. It can stand as "on land that was part of former rice plantations"; name True Blue and Midway only if the owner accepts secondary sources. Researcher note: Heritage history is from golf travel sites; the Heritage Club's own site was refused by the proxy. |

| 149 | (added 2026-10-14, round 10) Myrtle Beach Golf and Yacht Club is gated with a gatehouse at its entrance on Bay Road. Page facts line: "a gatehouse at the entrance on Bay Road; listings describe security at the gate day and night." | Gatehouse (aerial); entrance point (-79.043605, 33.647343) | USDA NAIP 2023 aerial (USGS National Map image service), close-up of the entrance; Horry County GIS roads layer 18; MLS listing (affordable ledger row 144) | https://imagery.nationalmap.gov/arcgis/rest/services/USGSNAIPImagery/ImageServer ; https://www.horrycounty.org/parcelapp/rest/services/HorryCountyGISApp/MapServer/18 ; https://www.tracimiles.com/listings/view/6622-cinnamon-fern-ln-myrtle-beach-sc-real-estate-2524896.html | Aerial: a small roofed building between the inbound and outbound lanes where Blue Heron Boulevard leaves Bay Road (the same kind of evidence as row 82, Prestwick). Close-up saved in the writer's scratchpad (gated-r10/mbgy-gate.png). Listing 2524896: "the security of a 24/7 staffed entry gate". | 2026-10-14 | 2027-10 | verified with corrected wording | 2026-10-14, round 10 independent verifier. B14 applied. AERIAL: opened the NAIP 2023 image at the entrance and Esri World Imagery (about 0.25 m a pixel). Both show a small roofed building on an island in the entrance road, about 15 m west of the label point (-79.043605, 33.647343). No gate arm and no person is visible, so the picture shows a building, not a gate or staff. NOAA 2026 tiles do not reach this latitude. PRIMARY SOURCES FOUND (the researcher had none): (1) The HOA's own site, mbgyc.com home page: 'Gatehouse Phone: (843) 650-4109' under 'Maint. & Security Info'; 'Community Watch Desk', 'Coverage is 24 Hours', 'Seven days a week'. It does not say where the gatehouse is or that it stands at the gate. (2) The recorded covenants on mbgyc.com (Amended and Restated Declaration dated 1985, amended 1991), Section 4.7: 'There is reserved unto Developer, the association and their respective successors and assigns the right and privilege, but not the obligation: (i) to maintain guarded or electronically monitored gates controlling vehicular access to and from the Subdivision'. Section 8.7: 'Each of the streets in the Subdivision ... is a private street'. A reserved right, not proof of a gate today. (3) Listings: 'Gated' in the MLS field of all 12 older-section listings (affordable row 144); 6622 Cinnamon Fern Ln (opened): 'an established, gated community', 'the security of a 24/7 staffed entry gate', fee $95 includes 'security'. No county record or news story about the gate was found. SUPPORTED: the community is gated; the HOA names a gatehouse; a small building stands at the Bay Road entrance. NOT SUPPORTED as fact: that anyone staffs the gate day and night (only listings say so; the HOA says only that a Community Watch Desk has 24-hour coverage). LIMIT: D.R. Horton's Sandpiper Place page calls Sandpiper Place itself 'gated' and its directions say 'Once you reach Myrtle Beach Golf and Yacht Club, turn left into the neighborhood'; whether Sandpiper Place is inside the Bay Road gate is not shown. Safe wording: 'Myrtle Beach Golf and Yacht Club has a gatehouse at its entrance off Bay Road [HOA website lists a gatehouse phone; 2023 aerial shows a small building there]. Listings describe 24-hour security at the gate [listings, 2026-10-14]'. The page facts line as written may stand. Researcher note: the listing is the seller's agent's words, so the page says 'listings describe' in the facts line only. The verifier should look at the NAIP close-up again and say whether the building is a gatehouse. Writer, 2026-10-14 (round 10b): facts line kept. |

Horton sales); beach 15 min | Affordable-places ledger (batch 2026-10-b) rows 120, 132, 144, 161 and 164 | batches/2026-10-b/facts/affordable-facts.md | Row 132: one-story houses, most built in the 1980s, about 1,350 square feet, HOA about $95 a month. Row 161: older part 35 sales, middle $225,000, all resales; Sandpiper Place 91 sales by D.R. Horton, middle 329,000; amenities reworded by that verifier: pool, tennis and shuffleboard courts, a playground and a clubhouse; storage area for RVs and trailers. Row 144: public boat ramp on the Intracoastal Waterway close by. Row 164: Golf and Yacht Club beach 15 minutes. | 2026-10-14 | 2027-10 | verified with corrected wording | 2026-10-14, round 10 independent verifier. Rests on affordable ledger rows 120, 132, 144, 161 and 164. Their own independent verifiers marked them verified or verified with corrected wording in rounds 8 and 8b, so the 'pending there too' note below is out of date. I did not re-pull the deed list. I re-checked what I could: listing 6622 Cinnamon Fern Ln (opened): built 1989, one story, fee $95 including security, pool, hot tub, playground, picnic area, pavilion, clubhouse, tennis and shuffleboard courts, 'a separate storage area on premises for RVs and trailers through the HOA'. County parcels: the association owns a 'POOL & TENNIS COURT' parcel of 4.05 acres. CORRECT 1: the page says 'The clubhouse has a pool, tennis and shuffleboard courts and a playground'. The listing names them as community amenities, not the clubhouse's. Say 'The community has a clubhouse, a pool, tennis and shuffleboard courts and a playground'. CORRECT 2 (distance word): 'A public boat ramp on the Intracoastal Waterway is close by' repeats listing words. Measured: Enterprise Landing (OSM node 4409738803, 33.66783, -79.06079) is 2.28 km (1.4 miles) from the middle of the older part (affordable row 178, which the round 10 affordable verifier re-ran) and 2.78 km (1.7 miles) from the Bay Road entrance. Safe wording: 'A public boat ramp on the Intracoastal Waterway, Enterprise Landing, is about a mile and a half away in a straight line'. UNSUPPORTED: the page's last paragraph 'Most houses here are older and smaller than in the other neighborhoods on this page.' No row compares house age or size across the page's 20 places, and the age half is doubtful: Myrtle Trace's first lot deeds are from November 1983 and Prestwick's course opened in 1989. What is supported: the older part's typical price, about $225,000 (35 resales), is the lowest on the page; Myrtle Trace is next at about $300,000. 'Most houses are one story, built in the 1980s, about 1,350 square feet' stands (row 132: 6 of 11 or 7 of 12 older houses built in the 1980s; heated size middle 1,338). Sandpiper Place middle $329,000 (91 D.R. Horton sales) and the beach at 15 minutes rest on affordable rows 161 and 164 (verified there). Researcher note: Those rows are pending there too; this page uses their verifier's wording (no hot tub). Writer, 2026-10-14 (round 10b): applied: 'The community has a clubhouse, a pool...'; Enterprise Landing 'a public boat ramp ... about a mile and a half away in a straight line'; 'older and smaller' cut, replaced by the Sandpiper Place gate line (row 175). |

DISTANCE WORDS: (1) 'less than half a mile away' holds from the middle of the neighborhood, in a straight line; from the far end of Seaside the Lowe's is 1,105 m (0.7 mile) and the Walmart 890 m. Say 'less than half a mile from the middle of the neighborhood'. (2) CORRECT: 'two or three blocks from the ocean' (page and heading 'a few blocks from the ocean'). Measured to the OSM coastline: the dot is 832 m (0.52 mile), the median Seaside parcel 779 m, the nearest parcel 367 m (0.23 mile). 'Two blocks' in 2617207 and 2513399 is blocks to the beach access, and 'three blocks from the Atlantic' is one listing (2621272). (3) 'Main Street is a short trip by golf cart': four listings say a golf cart ride (2617207, 2606635, 2618142, 2509083); Main Street is 724 m from the dot at its nearest point. (b) 'Both bring thousands of dancers to Main Street': row 115 supports only 'a local paper expected the Fall Migration to bring at least 10,000 people' (Horry Independent, 2023-09-07; I re-opened it: 'The Fall Migration is expected to bring at least 10,000 people to North Myrtle Beach'; the article has no 'thousands' and says Fun Monday is held on Main Street). The Spring Safari crowd has no source I could open (a search summary says organizers expected about 13,000; the paper returned 429). 'Many of its houses are brick' is 5 of 14 listings (2617207, 2611763, 2513399, 2513000, 2509083), and lake views 5 of 14: say 'some' for brick; 'many ... back up to its lakes' is the round 7 wording 'many homes are on its lakes' (5 listings); 'some look onto its lakes' is safer. B29 (aerial): county parcels drawn over the frame; Seaside Plantation fills the frame (Tilghman Estates adjoins it on the right, which is another subdivision). The 'Seaside Plantation' dot is a street junction inside Seaside parcels. The 'Lake' dot (OSM way 45443294, NHD) tests inside that polygon and sits on a small dark water arm; the two large lakes are elsewhere in the frame, so the label names a small piece of the lake system. It stands. Evidence: batches/2026-10-b/data/gated-amenities-mls.json (Seaside Plantation descriptions). Writer, 2026-10-14 (round 10b): applied: Tilghman Beach cut; 'a quarter to a half mile from the ocean' (heading 'a half mile'); Fall Migration line as the verifier gives it, linked; 'Some of its houses are all brick, and some look onto its lakes'; shops 'from the middle of the neighborhood'; facts line 'Six sales were recorded in the past year'; Main Street given as measured, 0.45 mile from the same point (row 174). |
| 154 | (added 2026-10-14, round 10) Grande Dunes: Burroughs & Chapin, which grew out of Myrtle Beach Farms, launched it; it stretches from the ocean to the Carolina Bays Preserve; its Resort Course follows the waterway along a bluff; its homes cost more than in any other neighborhood on the page but DeBordieu; most of Grande Dunes is open and only some sections have their own gate. | Prices on the page: DeBordieu $2 million, Grande Dunes $1.5 million, the rest $1.35 million or less | Rows 34, 117, 134, 145 (verified); the page's own price column | batches/2026-10-b/facts/gated-communities-facts.md | Row 117 (corrected): Burroughs & Chapin grew out of Myrtle Beach Farms; Resort Course on a bluff above the Intracoastal Waterway. Row 145: "Stretching from the Ocean to the Carolina Bays Preserve". Row 34: only some sections are gated. | 2026-10-14 | 2027-10 | wrong | 2026-10-14, round 10 independent verifier. Wording row. Four parts fail against the rows it cites. (1) 'its Resort Course follows the waterway along a bluff': row 117 (round 8 verifier) says drop 'bluff' (it appears only on golf travel sites; the course site returned 403) and row 134 gives 'runs along the Intracoastal Waterway'. The researcher's quote 'Resort Course on a bluff above the Intracoastal Waterway' is row 117's uncorrected claim. (2) 'it stretches from the ocean to the Carolina Bays Preserve': row 145 (round 9 verifier) says drop it: five listings from one agent's template, no official source names the preserve. The researcher quote in this row repeats it. B30 regression in both. (3) 'most of Grande Dunes is open and only some sections have their own gate': no row counts sections, lots or area. Row 34 (listings) shows Calais, Palermo, Tuscany Village and Villa Venezia gated on every listing read, Members Club 2 of 3 and Seville 1 of 2, Living Dunes 0 of 3 and Siena Park 0 of 2, and row 38 none on 19 Del Webb listings. A listing field that does not mark a gate is not proof of an open section, and 'most' cannot be counted. Safe wording: 'Only some sections of Grande Dunes are marked gated by listings: Calais, Palermo, Tuscany Village and Villa Venezia; listings for Living Dunes and Siena Park do not mark a gate [listings, 2026-10-13]'. (4) 'its homes cost more than in any other neighborhood on the page but DeBordieu': the $1.5 million is the middle of 11 sales in three sections (Calais, Palermo, Villa Venezia; row 46), not of Grande Dunes. Litchfield by the Sea has no single typical price (condos under $200,000 to over $2 million). Safe wording: 'Homes in its three gated sections sold for a middle price of about $1.5 million, the highest typical price on this page except DeBordieu's about $2 million [deed records, 11 sales]'. Also the page's 'Its gated sections of houses are Calais, Palermo and Villa Venezia' leaves out Tuscany Village (row 34); say 'include', and the facts line 'a gate at the entrance of each gated section' rests on listing fields only (Toll Brothers describes Cascadia alone as inside a staff-gated section, row 11); say 'listings mark these sections as gated'. Parts that hold: Burroughs & Chapin 'grew out of Myrtle Beach Farms' (row 117: SC Encyclopedia, 'later Burroughs & Chapin'); launched Grande Dunes and made its first sale in October 2000 (row 134); about 2,200 acres, trails, deep-water marina on the waterway, Ocean Club (rows 96, 145). B29: Villa Venezia dot is inside the Villa Venezia parcels; the Intracoastal Waterway dot is on the dark water (the picture shows Palermo at the left and the waterway across the right). Researcher note: Wording row; no new source. Writer, 2026-10-14 (round 10b): applied: 'runs along the Intracoastal Waterway'; preserve dropped; sections 'include Calais, Palermo, Tuscany Village and Villa Venezia'; 'Only some sections of Grande Dunes are gated; Living Dunes and Siena Park have no gate marked'; price is the middle of the three sections, 'among the highest on this page'; facts line 'listings mark ... as gated'. |

| 161 | (added 2026-10-14, round 10) Wachesaw Plantation: before the neighborhoods were planned, archaeologists dug there, among them James Michie of the University of South Carolina, who called it the most extensive archaeological research done on any South Carolina plantation; some finds are on display in the clubhouse; Arthur Lachiotte Jr. and Olan Mills II planned its neighborhoods in the early 1980s, and much of its land is kept in conservation; inside are Native American burial mounds, an Avenue of the Oaks, a Civil War-era fort and a cemetery of enslaved people; Kimbel's on the bluff was once a trading post where Waccamaw people and English colonists traded deer and beaver skins for beads and tools; the members own the club; an optional membership includes eight clay tennis courts and Tom Fazio's course; Magnolia's and other new amenities are due in the spring of 2027. | as quoted | Horry Independent / myhorrynews.com, Sarah Rose, 2026-07-07, "Wachesaw Plantation Club members invest in new amenities while preserving historic site" | https://www.myhorrynews.com/news/wachesaw-plantation-club-members-invest-in-new-amenities-while-preserving-historic-site/article_0f59c7a2-d445-4a63-9ffb-37a59e80efbf.html | "a members-owned golf and country club nestled in a beautiful residential community along the banks of the Waccamaw River"; "neighborhoods were designed in the early 1980s"; "different levels of membership, available for residents as well as nonresidents, include access to eight clay tennis courts and the Tom Fazio-designed 18-hole course"; "Native American burial mounds dating back 11,000 years, an Avenue of the Oaks, a Civil War-era fort and an enslaved cemetery that can still be visited today"; "relics and artifacts that archeologists, the late James Michie from the University of South Carolina Department of Archeology and Anthropology and Jay Mills excavated before the community was planned by founders and developers, 'Doc' Arthur Herbert Lachiotte, Jr. and Olan Mills II"; Kimbel's bluff "was once the site of an outpost where ... Waccamaw Native Americans and English colonists would have traded 'deer, beaver, fox and raccoon skins in return for glass beads, copper and brass bracelets, muskets, farm tools, salt and rum'"; Michie: "the most extensive archaeological research done on any South Carolina plantation"; "much of the community's ecology in conservation". | 2026-10-14 | 2027-10 | verified with corrected wording | 2026-10-14, round 10 independent verifier (rows 161 to 171). Opened the Horry Independent article (Sarah Rose, Jul 7, 2026) and the club's own Our Story page (wachesaw.com/Home/Our_Story). MATCH: members-owned club; neighborhoods 'designed in the early 1980s'; relics excavated 'before the community was planned by founders and developers, Doc Arthur Herbert Lachiotte, Jr. and Olan Mills II' by 'the late James Michie from the University of South Carolina ... and Jay Mills'; on display in the clubhouse (the club site: 'Many of the old artifacts can now be found within ... Clubhouse'); burial mounds, Avenue of the Oaks, Civil War-era fort, enslaved cemetery; eight clay tennis courts and the Tom Fazio-designed course; Magnolia's 'Completion is slated for the spring of 2027'. The club's own site backs only the survey (early 1980s), the burial mounds and the artifacts; Lachiotte, the fort, the Avenue, the cemetery and the outpost rest on the article alone (a lifestyle piece quoting the club president; not checked against Michie's 1987 USC report). MUST CHANGE (sensitive history, stay inside the source): (1) 'Kimbel's restaurant is on the bluff above the river. It was once a trading post, where Waccamaw people and English colonists traded deer and beaver skins for beads and tools.' is WRONG: the article says the BLUFF 'was once the site of an outpost where, according to [two local historians], Waccamaw Native Americans and English colonists would have traded deer, beaver, fox and raccoon skins in return for glass beads, copper and brass bracelets, muskets, farm tools, salt and rum', 'would have', and 'outpost' not 'trading post'. Safe wording: 'The bluff where Kimbel's restaurant stands was once the site of an outpost where, by one local history, Waccamaw Native Americans and English colonists would have traded animal skins for beads, tools, salt and rum [Horry Independent, 2026-07-07, citing Kolenick and Petrone]'. (2) 'Inside the gates are Native American burial mounds...': the article says 'the property', never the gates; and it adds the cemetery 'can still be visited today', which says nothing about gate access. Say 'On the property are'. (3) 'He called it the most extensive archaeological research done on any South Carolina plantation': Michie's words are 'This project has resulted in the most extensive archaeological research done on any South Carolina plantation'; 'it' reads as the plantation. One source for a superlative: write 'Michie said the project was the most extensive archaeological research done on any South Carolina plantation' or use the club's 'one of the most thoroughly researched plantations in the South'. (4) 'much of its land is kept in conservation': the article says 'much of the community's ecology in conservation'. Say 'much of the community's natural land is in conservation' or quote 'ecology'. (5) 'A membership, which is optional, includes eight clay tennis courts and Tom Fazio's course': the article says 'different levels of membership, available for residents as well as nonresidents, include access to' both. Say 'Memberships come in levels, open to residents and nonresidents, and give access to eight clay tennis courts and Tom Fazio's course'. (6) 'The club is still building ... with completion slated for the spring of 2027': the article is Jul 7, 2026 and the 2027 date is for the pool-side Magnolia's; the pool complex is separate ('grounds are being prepared'). Say 'Construction began in 2026 on a new pool-side restaurant, Magnolia's, due in the spring of 2027 [Horry Independent, 2026-07-07]'. NOTE: the club's own site says Kimbels 'overlooks the Intracoastal Waterway'; 'above the river' (row 126 wording) can stand. The page never states the 11,000-year figure: keep it out. Opened wachesaweast.com (current): About says 'Playing in the 1998 City of Hope Classic, LPGA Tour player Meg Mallon set the course record: 10-under par 62'; the course page says the signature hole is the 18th ('signature 18th hole ... around a picturesque pond'); the dining page names the grill 'The Toasted Tee Pub & Grub'. (4) 'In 1998, Meg Mallon shot a 62, then an LPGA record': only the 2013 GM says 'an all time LPGA scoring record'; the independent record (Mickey Wright's 1964 62, Wikipedia; Se Ri Pak's first 61 in July 1998, Golf Compendium) shows 62 was the LPGA 18-hole record then, so Mallon's 62 matched it rather than set it. Not verified. Safe wording: 'In 1998, Meg Mallon shot a 62 here, the course record [Wachesaw East site]'. | 164 | (added 2026-10-14, round 10) Willbrook Plantation: part of a grant of 24,000 acres made in 1711; 149 enslaved people counted in 1850; around 1900 hurricanes wrecked the rice fields' dikes; archaeologists surveyed the land before Dan Maples' course opened; open fields cut by straight ditches lie along its west side; the beach is outside the gate, a golf cart ride away at Litchfield by the Sea. | OSM waterway=ditch ways (for example 45308758) in the fields | Row 129 verifier's primary source (Chicora Foundation Research Series 31); row 107; OpenStreetMap | https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/45308758 | Row 129 verifier: "originally part of a 24,000 acre tract granted by the Lords Proprietors to Landgrave Robert Daniel on June 18, 1711"; 1850: 149 enslaved people (Table 1); hurricanes of 1893 to 1911 wrecked the dike system; Chicora survey 1987 before the course. Course marker article (myrtlebeachgolftrips.com, read by WebFetch): slave cemetery marker on the 8th hole, 'black slaves who labored on Oatland Plantation'; 'brick remnants that remain visible today' mark the site of a house built after the 1895 fire. (3) 'the brick remains of the plantation house': the bricks mark a house built after the 1895 fire; say 'brick remains at the site of the plantation house'. Source for (2) and (3) is the course marker, via a golf-package site (secondary). (4) 'The beach is outside the gate.' no source; read as 'owners cannot use it'. Row 107: say only 'The beach and crabbing docks of Litchfield by the Sea are a golf cart ride away [listings]'. 'Open fields, cut by straight ditches' holds on the aerial (checked with the parcel overlay). Built the page in a scratch copy of the site (tools/mkpage.js with the batch's specs and website patches; no repo file touched) and read the output. Title and og:title 'Gated Communities in Myrtle Beach: 20 Neighborhoods'; meta description and sub 'Twenty'; short answer 'Of the 20 on this page'; glance 'Ten of the 20'; table caption 'All 20'; 20 numbered H2s; the maps (alt text 'Neighborhoods 1 to 14' and '15 to 20', key start=15, SVG dots 1 to 20, LBTS dot 18); JSON-LD ItemList numberOfItems 20 with 20 elements; FAQ lists ten golf names. All agree. Golf list (ten): Tidewater, Barefoot, Grande Dunes, Prestwick, Wachesaw, Wachesaw East, The Reserve, Willbrook, Heritage, DeBordieu; the 'What are you looking for' table lists the same ten; ocean two. Golf and Yacht Club: it had a golf course (WMBF: the community board announced its closing); row 171 says 'no course', which holds only for now. The page never says it has none; keep it that way. FEE: all 19 priced neighborhoods fall between $90 and $400, Litchfield by the Sea has no figure, so 'in most' holds. PRICE: 'homes sell for about $225,000 to $2 million' leaves out Litchfield by the Sea's condos from under $200,000 to over $2 million; 'in most' covers it. STALE COMMENTS in the spec (not page text): lines 8 to 9, 136 to 137 and 168 still say 19 places and '14 to 19'. Not a page fault.
